Graded Base system

Graded substitution is admissible, akin to the second clause
of Linear Base substitution (Lemma
2.8
):

Lemma 2.11 (Admissibility of substitution).

If
\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{A}
and

\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}
then

{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot}\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}
.

Example 2.12.

The simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus is recovered
by instantiating Graded Base with the single-point semiring
\mathcal{R}=\{\bullet\}
.

Example 2.13.

The higher-order function
h=\lambda\mathit{f}.\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{f}\,(\mathit{f}\,\mathit{x})
mentioned in the introduction, composing a function with
itself twice, has the following derivation for its body:

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\;}{\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A})\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\frac{\frac{\;}{\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A})\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\frac{\;}{\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}),\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}\,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right={app${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\hskip-30.00005pt}{\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}(1+1)}}(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}),\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}(\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{r})}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}\,(\mathit{f}\,\mathit{x}):\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right={app${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}

All the semirings mentioned in Section
2.1
(Linear Base) can also be used with
Graded Base.

Operational Semantics and Equational theory

Figure
4
(second group) gives the
operational semantics, which is that of the CBN

\lambda
-calculus. Appendix
A
gives the definition of syntactic
substitution which is standard.

Figure
4
(third group) defines an equational theory
for the calculus, giving the key rules but omitting the congruence
rules which apply to all sub-term positions. Note
\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}}\,\subseteq\,\equiv_{\textsc{g}}
.

2.3.
Translation

In the course of the paper, we define translations between several calculi of the Linear and Graded
strands, according to the overview in Figure
1
. As for the term and type syntax
of the different calculi, we use the same interpretation brackets across different variations of
linear and graded calculi respectively, making clear from context which interpretation we are referring to.

The interpretation

{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}-{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
takes graded terms to linear terms and
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt-\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
takes linear
terms to graded terms. The following mnemonics have proven useful to the authors:

•

Given a graded term
t
,
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
is its translation, into a
L
inear term,
with the square
L
ilac brackets mirroring the shape of the letter L.

•

Given a linear term
t
,
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
is its translation into a
G
raded term,
with the rounded
G
reen parentheses mirroring the shape of the letter G.

The same notation is used for type and context translations.

2.3.1.
Graded Base to Linear Base translation

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{x}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\mathit{x}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\qquad\mathit{y}\,\#\,\mathit{t}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Term translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\emptyset\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\emptyset\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\Delta,\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket},\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Context translation)}\end{array}}

The key idea is to use the graded modality to capture the grading of assumptions and functions.
This translation leads to the insight that Graded Base effectively
wraps a graded modality around function inputs, capturing the usage of the parameter in the body.
Since every term in Graded Base has an associated grading in the context,
every abstraction in the translation from Graded to Linear needs to do an unboxing (let) and every application needs to do a boxing (promotion).
The graded function type
\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}
translates to the Linear Base function type
\multimap
, with its left-hand side
under
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}
.

Contexts are translated straightforwardly. The translation can never produce a linear assumption.

Example 2.14.

The judgment
\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}(1+1)}}(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}),\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}(\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{r})}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}\,(\mathit{f}\,\mathit{x}):\mathit{A}

in Graded Base translates to
\mathit{f}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{(1+1)}}},\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{(\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{r})}}}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}:{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

in Linear Base. Note the right-hand sides of an application find themselves under a promotion.

Remark 2.15.

We do not translate
\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}
to a linear function
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
;
this is because
\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}
does not necessarily mean that the function uses its argument linearly.
Some choices of semiring mean that
1
does not mean ‘used exactly once’, the simplest example
being the single-point (trivial) semiring in which
0=1
and so
1
would mean any usage.

We now establish key soundness results. Firstly, that the translation is type
preserving, i.e., translation of terms,
contexts, and types is such that a well-typed derivation in Graded
Base is well-typed under its translation to Linear Base. This
requires various small lemmas that the translation is a
homomorphism between graded-base contexts and linear-base contexts
which we elide here. Secondly, that the translation
preserves the operational semantics, and lastly that it preserves the
equational theory. Appendix
B.1
provides the full proofs and supporting lemmas.

{thrm}
[][Soundness of the Graded Base to Linear Base translation]

•

Type preservation:
\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}:{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

•

Operational correspondence:
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

•

Equation preservation:
\mathit{t}\equiv_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\,\equiv_{\textsc{l}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

2.3.2.
Linear Base to Graded Base translation

A type-preserving translation from Linear Base to Graded Base is however more difficult
since we need to find some encoding of the graded modal type
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
.
From the introduction rule of the graded modality (promotion), such a translation on
terms should incur a multiplication of the context by
r
which in Graded Base is only
provided through application of a
\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}
function. Thus a translation must have such
a function in a negative position, suggesting a continuation-passing style (CPS)
or Church-encoding-like form.

Taking inspiration from
28
on encoding products,
we extend Plotkin’s CPS translation of the call-by-name
\lambda
-calculus
(
41
)

with a CPS encoding of the graded modalities of Linear Base into Graded Base:

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;(({\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;({\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathrm{K})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathrm{K}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;(\mathrm{K}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{k}.\mathit{x}\,\mathit{k}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{k}.\mathit{k}\,(\lambda\mathit{x}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis})\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{k}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,(\lambda\mathit{f}.\mathit{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,\mathit{k})\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{k}.\mathit{k}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{k}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,(\lambda\mathit{x}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,\mathit{k})\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Term translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\emptyset{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\emptyset\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Context translation)}\end{array}}

The key point is to (recursively) encode
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}
as

(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathrm{K})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathrm{K}
so that in the case
of promotion
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}
we can use application to scale
the grades of
\mathit{t}
. Note that linear assumptions get mapped
to graded assumptions with grade
1
, though this may not mean
‘linear use’ (see Section
3.2.2
for
a solution).

{thrm}

[][Soundness of the Linear Base to Graded Base translation (proofs in Appendix
B.2
).]

•

Type preservation:
\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}:{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

•

Operational correspondence:
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\rightsquigarrow_{\beta\textsc{g}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

•

Equation preservation:
\mathit{t}\equiv^{\beta_{\to}\beta\eta_{\Box}}_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\equiv_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

As we use Plotkin’s CBN CPS translation, there are two implications of note here:
Firstly, regarding operational correspondence, our source uses CBN but we need full

\beta
-reduction (via the
\textsc{congAbs}_{\textsf{G}}
rule) in the target to prove simulation.
Computational adequacy then follows from the
\lambda
-calculus standardisation theorem.
Secondly, regarding equation preservation: for the
\lambda
-calculus subset, we
preserve only
\beta
-equality for functions, and not
\eta
-equality—a well-known limitation of Plotkin’s translation.
However, we preserve both
\beta
- and
\eta
-equality for the graded modality,
hence we label the equational theory here as
\equiv^{\beta_{\to}\beta\eta_{\Box}}_{\textsc{l}}
.

In Section
4
, we consider a polymorphic Graded Base but
instead for the moment, we consider a variant calculus that is also well-used in the
literature: a ‘Graded Modal Base’.

3.
Extending Graded Base with a Graded Modality

We now add a graded modality to
Graded Base thereby arriving at
Graded Modal Base
. This captures the type system
features of calculi like
GraD

(
13
)
and a monomorphic
subset of
1
, and permits a simple translation to Linear Base.

3.1.
Graded Modal Base

Graded Modal Base can now share the syntax (and operational semantics) of Linear Base:

(shared terms)

\displaystyle\mathit{t}

\displaystyle::=\mathit{x}\mid\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\mid\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\mid\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}

(extended types)

\displaystyle\mathit{A}

\displaystyle::=\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\mid\mathrm{K}

Typing judgments in Graded Modal Base are of the form
\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}
.
The typing rules are the same as Graded Base with addition of typing for
graded modal introduction and elimination:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\\
\end{array}}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}}\;\textsc{{{pr${}_{\textsf{G}}$}}{}}}\quad{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{cc}\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}&\;\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\end{array}\\
\end{array}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}\;\textsc{{{let${}_{\textsf{G}}$}}{}}}\end{array}

The equational theory extends to include
\beta\eta
-equality on
graded modalities (see Appendix
A.5
).

3.2.
Translation

3.2.1.
Linear Base to Graded Modal Base translation

We can now define the translation from Linear Base to Graded
Modal
Base,
which on terms is a homomorphism:

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\mathit{x}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{x}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}={\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Term translation)}\\[5.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathrm{K}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\\[5.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\emptyset{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\emptyset\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Context translation)}\end{array}}

For types, we translate
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}
in the straightforward way, directly
to the Graded Modal Base graded modality. To translate
the linear function space we grade the resulting function arrow
in Graded Modal Base at
1\in\mathcal{R}
and similarly for linear assumptions.

Example 3.1.

The following Linear Base judgment types a
K
-combinator-like expression:

\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{0}}\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}

The parameter
y
is used by eliminating the graded modality,
binding to
z
but then discarding the term. Its translation to Graded Modal Base results
in an identical term but with translated types:

\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{0}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

{thrm}

[][Soundness of the Linear Base to Graded Modal Base translation]

•

Type preservation:
\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}:{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

•

Operational correspondence:
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

•

Equation preservation:
\mathit{t}\,\equiv_{\textsc{l}}\,\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\equiv_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

Appendix
B.3
provides the proof along with its supporting lemmas.

Remark 3.2.

We might consider whether there is a dual to the above, a kind of completeness result,
i.e., that
given
\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}

there exists
\Gamma,\mathit{t^{\prime}},\mathit{A^{\prime}}

such that
\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{A^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}:{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

and
\Delta\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

and
\mathit{t}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

and
\mathit{A}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
.
However, an easy counterexample is the following judgment:

\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}0}}\mathit{B}.

There is no type in Linear Base which translates to
\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}0}}\mathit{B}
as we always
translate the linear function type to
\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}
. Indeed, the analogous
term in Linear Base was already shown in Example
3.1
, where
we see that the result of the translation has type
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{0}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
.

Thus, the translation from Linear Base to Graded Modal Base is not surjective. Neither
is it injective: both the linear assumption
\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}
and the graded assumption

\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{1}}}
are translated to
\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
. For those semirings
in which
1
does not capture linearity (e.g., the singleton semiring, any semiring
where
0\leq 1
, and others) then linear and non-linear
use is conflated by this translation.

3.2.2.
A Linearity-Preserving Linear Base to Graded Modal Base Translation

Instead, we can give an alternate translation which is injective
and
linearity preserving
: keeping the translation of terms
as above, we can translate types and grades from Linear Base with semiring
\mathcal{R}

into types and grades in Graded Modal Base with semiring
(\mathcal{R}\times\{0,1,\omega\})
, i.e., the product semiring (Example
2.7
)
of
\mathcal{R}
with the linearity-capturing semiring (Example
2.5
).

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\langle 1,1\rangle}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\langle\mathit{r},\omega\rangle}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\mathrm{K}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}&\triangleq&\;\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\\[10.00002pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\langle 1,1\rangle}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma,\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\langle\mathit{r},\omega\rangle}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Context translation)}\end{array}}

Thus, the translation is now injective in the types since we translate a linear assumption
\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}
to
have grade
\langle 1,1\rangle
but a graded assumption
\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}
to
\langle\mathit{r},\omega\rangle

which is a different grade even if
r=1
. By taking the pairing with the linearity semiring,
we can capture the combination of linearity and grading that occurs in Linear Base, but
now in the Graded Modal Base setting.

Operational correspondence and equation preservation hold as before in Theorem
3.2.1
since the term translation is unchanged. We then re-restablish
type preservation of the translation:

{thrm}
[][Soundness of the Linearity-Preserving Linear Base to Graded Modal Base translation]

•

Type preservation:
\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}:{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}

Appendix
B.4
provides the proof.

The key insight in this translation is that Linear Base essentially comprises two parallel
forms of analysis: a graded analysis and a latent linear analysis witnessed by the
corresponding two forms of typing assumption in Linear Base contexts.
Our translation here captures these two aspects systematically,
by taking the product of the same grading semiring, and the linearity semiring.

A question may arise here:

What is the meaning of a grade such as
\langle 2,1\rangle
,
where the first component looks non-linear in
\mathcal{R}

but the second represents linearity?
Such a grade is not
in the image of our translation but we may still consider what it is
like to type terms in Graded Base with
\mathcal{R}\times\{0,1,\omega\}
. First, let us decompose
\langle 2,1\rangle

into
\langle 1+1,1\rangle

for clarity, i.e., for any semiring
\mathcal{R}
in the first component.
Such grades could be formed via approximation if

\mathcal{R}
permits
1\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\sqsubseteq}\,1+1
and thus
\langle 1,1\rangle\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\sqsubseteq}\,\langle 1+1,1\rangle

is an over-approximation of grades, e.g., useful for when calculating the grade
across multiple program branches. Without such an
approximation,
\langle 1+1,1\rangle
cannot be derived from variable use
and contraction of variables alone since in
(\mathcal{R}\times\{0,1,\omega\})
we have

\langle 1,1\rangle+\langle 1,1\rangle=\langle 1+1,\omega\rangle
.
Such a grade could only arise from an open term applying
a function, e.g.,

\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\langle 1+1,1\rangle}}\mathit{B},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\langle 1+1,1\rangle}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}}\mathit{f}\,\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}.

However there is no well-typed closed term that can be formed to substitute into

f
since we cannot form
\langle 1+1,1\rangle
without use of such an open term.
Consequently, for closed terms, we cannot form
\langle 1+1,1\rangle

unless it is permitted by approximation in
\mathcal{R}
.
In the approximation case, it is up to the choice (or design) of semiring
to permit approximation via the definition of
\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\sqsubseteq}\,
.

More generally, we may consider the advantage of pairing
semirings with the linearity semiring. Consider a semiring such as the security
lattice semiring (Example
2.6
) where
1=\mathsf{Lo}
.
Since
\mathsf{Lo}+\mathsf{Lo}=\mathsf{Lo}
and
0\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\sqsubseteq}\,\mathsf{Lo}
then a
\mathsf{Lo}
-graded variable
can represent both linear or non-linear usage. The pairing

\langle\mathsf{Lo},1\rangle
then distinguishes a linear low-security variable
from
\langle\mathsf{Lo},\omega\rangle
which instead represents a non-linear low security variable.
Conversely, recall that
0=\mathsf{Hi}
,
giving the translation
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathsf{Hi}}}\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\langle\mathsf{Hi},\omega\rangle}}\mathit{A}
,
which allows unlimited usage, but limited to
\mathsf{Hi}
security contexts.

Our approach here is not necessarily the only sound and injective translation,
however it seems particularly illuminating,
in that the product construction clearly demarcates and represents the two analyses present within Linear Base:
the explicit coeffect in the grades on the one hand and the latent linearity coeffect of the
system (not present in Graded Base) on the other.

3.2.3.
Graded Modal Base to Linear Base Translation

We can now extend the previous translation from Graded Base to Linear Base
(Section
2.3
) to translate from Graded Modal Base to Linear Base:

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{x}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\mathit{x}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\lambda\mathit{y}.\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\qquad\mathit{y}\,\#\,\mathit{t}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}={\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Term translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\mathrm{K}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\\[7.5pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\emptyset\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;\emptyset\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt\\
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\hskip 0.7pt\Delta,\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}&\triangleq&\;{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket},\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Context translation)}\end{array}}

The only new parts are the translation of the graded modality which maps directly
onto the graded modality in Linear Base. We then (re)establish soundness. Appendix
B.5
provides the proofs.

{thrm}
[][Soundness of the Graded Modal Base to Linear Base translation]

•

Type preservation:
\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\Delta{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}:{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

•

Operational correspondence:
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}\Box}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

•

Equation preservation:
\mathit{t}\,\equiv_{\textsc{g}_{\Box}}\,\mathit{t^{\prime}}\implies{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\,\equiv_{\textsc{l}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
.

3.2.4.
Mutual relationship

Given the pair of translations here, we might wonder: what is the relationship between
the translations? Are they mutually inverse? By considering just the translation of function
types we can quickly see that the translations are not inverse:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{B}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

Similarly, given
\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}
in Linear Base
then its translation to Graded Modal Base is
\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}

which is translated back to Linear Base as
\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}
.

However, by composing operational correspondence theorems, then round-tripping the translations
yields a term that corresponds operationally to the original, in both directions:

Corollary 3.3 (Observational isomorphism).

If
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}
then
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{l}}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t^{\prime}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

and if
\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}\Box}\mathit{t^{\prime}}
then
{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}\Box}^{\ast}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}\mathit{t^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
.

We might also wonder if there is an adjoint relationship between the translations. If
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}-{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\dashv{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt-\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
then we would expect to be able to construct a mapping in the category of types (whose morphisms are derivations of judgments with source as inputs and the target as the result):

\eta_{A}:A\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}

However, there is no such mapping for
\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{B}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
in general since this is undefined for a higher-order type.

Consider
(\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K})\multimap\mathrm{K}
for the base type
\mathrm{K}
whose translation between
the systems is the identity, and thus
{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}=\mathrm{K}
. Then
\eta
would need to provide
a derivation of the judgment:

\displaystyle(\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K})\multimap\mathrm{K}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K})\multimap\mathrm{K}

Such a derivation would require us to map a value of type
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K})

to one of type
\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
. We can certainly derive
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K})\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
by elimination
and dereliction, but
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}\not\vdash\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
. To see why, consider
the single-point semiring
0=1
and thus a function typed
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
may not use its argument
at all, whereas
\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
must use its argument exactly once.

Given the above negative result, we could then consider the opposite adjoint relationship, i.e., perhaps

{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt-\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\dashv{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}-{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
? In which case, we would require the counit
operation:

\epsilon_{A}:{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\to A

However, there is no such mapping, even in a first-order case: for

{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\llbracket}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathrm{K}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.4922,0.3047,0.6016}\rrbracket}
we need to
map to
\mathrm{K}\multimap\mathrm{K}
but as described above, no such derivation is possible.

Thus the translations are neither inverses nor adjoint.

3.3.
Generalised
\Box
-elimination

Elimination of graded modalities in both systems looks almost identical,
eliminating a graded modality at grade
r
by a kind of
‘cut’ (substitution) against a variable with matching grade
r
:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{cc}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}&\;\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{A}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]_{\mathit{r}}}}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\end{array}\\
\end{array}}{\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{l}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}\;\textsc{{{let${}_{\textsf{L}}$}}{}}}\quad{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{cc}\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}&\;\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\end{array}\\
\end{array}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}\;\textsc{{{let${}_{\textsf{G}}$}}{}}}

Some graded-base systems in the literature however
have a more general elimination rule
(
1
;
34
)
,
of the form:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{cc}\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}&\;\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}(\mathit{s}\cdot\mathit{r})}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\end{array}\\
\end{array}}{({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{s}\cdot}\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}})+\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}\;\textsc{{{let${}_{\textsf{G}}^{\prime}$}}{}}}

The generalised rule allows an additional scaling of the resources
for
\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
due to additional use of
x
in
\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. Such
a rule has not appeared in any Linear Base system, but could
easily be added to both systems to provide equivalent power
across both flavours of coeffect types.

This generalised typing enables the
derivation of a function
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A})\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{(\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s})}}\mathit{A}
,
akin to the multiplication (join) of a graded monad (the dual to comultiplication
of a graded comonad structure):

\displaystyle\vskip-10.00002pt\frac{\frac{\;}{\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A})\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{x}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A})}\quad\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\frac{\frac{\;}{\mathit{y}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{y}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\frac{\frac{\;}{\mathit{z}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{z}:\mathit{A}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{\mathit{z}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathit{z}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right={pr${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\hskip-19.66667pt}{\mathit{y}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}\hskip-24.58342pt}\quad\textsc{right={let${}_{\textsf{G}}^{\prime}$}{}}\hskip-30.00005pt}{\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A})\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\square}}\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{y}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{x}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{y}\,\mathsf{in}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{z}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right={let${}_{\textsf{G}}^{\prime}$}{}}

Indeed, the generalised
\Box
-elimination can be modelled in a
categorical semantics by a graded comonad along with an additional
graded multiplication operation
\mu:{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}({\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{s}}}\mathit{A})\multimap{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{(\mathit{r}\cdot\mathit{s})}}\mathit{A}
(as one might find in a graded monad structure).
However, not all graded comonads modelling
{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}

admit such an operation and so having the generalised

\Box
-elimination may not be desirable in general graded comonadic calculi.
We thus omitted this generalised rule from the main part of our results, considering
the simpler
let
{}_{\textsf{L}}
and
let
{}_{\textsf{G}}
rules.

4.
Deriving a Graded Modality with a Polymorphic Graded Base

5
develop an extension to GHC/Haskell that
provides a notion of linear typing by using a graded type system. The
core calculus is akin to that of Graded Base, but specialised to
the
\{0,1,\omega\}
semiring (Example
2.5
). Their calculus has no graded
modality, but the implementation as part of GHC/Haskell can
encode
the
unrestricted
modality

Ur
via an algebraic datatype:
1
1

1

https://hackage.haskell.org/package/linear-base-0.4.0/docs/Data-Unrestricted-Linear.html#t:Ur

⬇

Ur

::

a

->

Ur

a

where the arrow
\rightarrow
denotes an
\omega
-graded arrow for the two-point semiring

\{1,\omega\}
(
0
is not available in the implementation)
and thus this constructor is essentially of type
A\xrightarrow{\omega}\mathsf{Ur}\ A
in the Graded Base syntax.
25
give a generalisation of this definition to a graded modality:

⬇

Box

::

a

%
r

->

Box

r

a

This suggests an alternate Graded Base calculus in relation to Linear Base.
We previously showed (Section
2.3.2
) that a type-preserving translation of Linear Base terms into
Graded Base requires a CPS encoding. By changing the substrate of Graded Base to System F, we have

Graded Poly Base
, unlocking the ability for a direct-style
polymorphic Church encoding of this
Box
data type:

(1)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}=\forall\beta.(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\beta

This enables a less intrusive translation, without the need to CPS translate all terms.

4.1.
Graded Poly Base

We thus extend Graded Base with polymorphic typing, the syntax and
typing of which is standard:

(Terms)

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle::=\ldots\mid\mathit{t}\text{@}\mathit{A}\mid\Lambda\alpha.\mathit{t}

(Types)

\displaystyle\mathit{A}

\displaystyle::=\ldots\mid\alpha\mid\forall\alpha.\mathit{B}

where
\alpha,\beta
range over type variables, with
a corresponding standard substitution at the type-level
written
[\mathit{A}/\alpha]\mathit{B}
.
Typing judgments are of the form
\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}
extending
the previous typing with:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\\
\end{array}}{\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\Lambda\alpha.\mathit{t}:\forall\alpha.\mathit{A}}\;\textsc{{{tyabs${}_{\textsf{G}}$}}{}}}\quad{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t}:\forall\alpha.\mathit{A}\\
\end{array}}{\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t}\text{@}\mathit{B}:[\mathit{B}/\alpha]\mathit{A}}\;\textsc{{{tyapp${}_{\textsf{G}}$}}{}}}

The operational semantics has the standard
\beta
rule for type abstraction/application
and congruence:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\end{array}}{(\Lambda\alpha.\mathit{t})\text{@}\mathit{B}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}[\mathit{B}/\alpha]\mathit{t}}\;\textsc{{$\beta_{\text{{}}}$}{}}}\quad{\displaystyle\frac{\begin{array}[]{c}\mathit{t}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\\
\end{array}}{\mathit{t}\text{@}\mathit{A}\rightsquigarrow_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}\text{@}\mathit{A}}\;\textsc{{$\textsc{cong}${}}{}}}

Introduction and elimination for the derived
\Box_{r}
(from (
1
))
can therefore be derived respectively as follows (with their typing derivations shown):

(
\Box_{i}
)

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\frac{\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{\hskip 7.3612pt}{\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{f}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta^{\prime}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{Right={var${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot}\Delta,\mathit{f}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{f}\,\mathit{t}:\beta^{\prime}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{Right={app${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\lambda\mathit{f}.\mathit{f}\,\mathit{t}:(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta^{\prime})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\beta^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{Right={abs${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}\cdot}\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\Lambda\beta.\lambda\mathit{f}.\mathit{f}\,\mathit{t}:\forall\beta.(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\beta}\quad\textsc{Right={tyabs${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\qquad

(
\Box_{e}
)

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\forall\beta.(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\beta}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\text{@}\mathit{B}:(\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B})\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{Right={tyapp${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\quad\quad\frac{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\mathit{x}:_{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{Right={abs${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}}{\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}+\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\textsc{g}_{\forall}}(\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\text{@}\mathit{B})\,(\lambda\mathit{x}.\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{Right={app${}_{\textsf{G}}$}{}}\quad

4.1.1.
Graded Poly Base to Linear Base Translation

We thus adapt the translation of types to:

\displaystyle{\begin{array}[]{lcl@{\hspace{2em}}r}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\square_{\mathit{r}}}\mathit{A}{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}&\triangleq&\;\forall\beta.({\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{A}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}\mathit{r}}}\beta)\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}1}}\beta\hfil\hskip 20.00003pt&\text{(Type translation)}\end{array}}

We might expect that we can interpret the syntax of terms then directly
as in the above derivations, i.e.,

{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{t}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\triangleq\Lambda\beta.\lambda\mathit{f}.\mathit{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathsf{let}\,{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}[}\mathit{x}{\color[rgb]{0.0273,0.3125,0.8164}]}=\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\triangleq({\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}\text{@}\mathit{B})\,(\lambda\mathit{x}.{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\hskip 0.7pt\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 0.7pt{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis})
.
However, note the presence of
B
on the right-hand side of
\Box
-elimination,
which has now appeared out of nowhere. Instead the translation must
ingest the entire
typing derivation
, not merely the syntax of terms and types
separately. We can however translate the types separately.

Thus, we define the translation as mapping from typing derivations to terms,
where we denote derivation trees by
\Pi
:

(Term translation)

\displaystyle\;\triangleq\;\Lambda\beta.\lambda\mathit{f}.\mathit{f}\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\llparenthesis}\Pi{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4453,0.1016}\rrparenthesis}
